He has no spouse or offspring. [ CAS Pnom ] He has ludicrously short legs, exceedingly elongated arms, and a round, sleepy-looking head hanging from a spherical body, as if it were turning a somnambulistic somersault. His furriness and somnolent expression give him a vague though inverted likeness to Tsathoggua . His locomotion is mainly on his hands, for the absurd legs are not half long enough for the steps he has to take. Hzioulquoigmnzhah emigrated to Yaksh Neptune ) at an early age to escape Cxaxukluth's cannibalistic tendencies. Wearying of the peculiar worship rendered by the Yakshians, Hzioulquoigmnzhah emigrated again, this time to Cykranosh. He was long worshipped by the quaint peoples of Cykranosh but grew tired of them and had retired from active life by the time of his encounter with the wizard Eibon .

--- Abholos.txt ---

[1] Ghisguth L65
НАЗВАНИЕ: Abholos ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:11:25 СИМВОЛОВ: 810 Abholos Also known as Devourer in the Mist Relatives Tsathoggua (possible brother) Cthulhu (possible parent) Affiliations Great Old Ones First appearance EXP Stunning Eldritch Tales Created by Robin Laws Abholos (also known as Devourer in the Mist ) is a fictional entity created by Robin Laws for the Trail of Cthulhu gaming supplement Stunning Eldritch Tales Overview Abholos is a Great Old One who appears to be a great grey, festering glob of endless malevolence. According to one account, he is a product of Ghisguth and Zstylzhemghi mating, which also gave birth to Tsathoggua . This made Abholos more of a self-willed placenta that had taken form in-and-of-itself, rather than a brother or cousin of the others. According to another account, Abholos is a spawn of Cthulhu , born from his bile and tears.

Ithaqua is especially associated with icy, snowy air. ][ Hastur and Lloigor are especially associated with outer space. Hastur is of interplanetary spaces, and Lloigor is the Star Treader. Lloigor and Ithaqua both sometimes seize humans. Ithaqua takes them on long journeys before finally dropping them from the sky. Ithaqua transports his victims along with their clothing ][ , and even allows them to collect momentos on their journeys . On the other hand, Lloigor draws the body from the earth piecemeal, and leaves the victim's empty clothes behind, as if the body were drawn or sucked out of them The Air beings oppose other evil ones that are associated with the Water element .

--- Alfred Clarendon.txt ---

[1] Nug and Yeb L81
His closest friend from childhood, who called him "Little Alf", was James Dalton, his protector at Phillips Exeter and Columbia University. He travels throughout the world to study diseases, including to North Africa, the Middle East, India, and China , including Tibet. In Tibet's U-Tsang, he acquires a team of eight servants of "skeletonic leanness" and "grotesque" aspect, whom he dresses in "the loose black silk robes of Bonpa priests". In the Hoggar Mountains of Algeria, he meets the mysterious Surama , who becomes his chief factotum and clinic-man. --- All-Consuming Fire.txt ---

[7] Lloigor L385
P. Lovecraft's The Whisperer in Darkness Behind the Mythos Elements from the Cthulhu Mythos had previously been incorporated into the Doctor Who universe in the novel White Darkness , by David A. McIntee. However, All-Consuming Fire is notable for identifying several monsters from the original TV series as classic Mythos entities. For instance, the Animus from the serial The Web Planet is implied to be Lloigor , whereas Fenric from the serial The Curse of Fenric is identified as Hastur the Unspeakable , and the Doctor's recurring enemy, the Great Intelligence , is identified as Yog-Sothoth . Additionally, the unnamed Great Old One that the Doctor faced in White Darkness is retroactively identified as Cthulhu . Other entities referred to as Great Old Ones in the novel include Nyarlathotep Dagon , and the Gods of Ragnarok (from The Greatest Show in the Galaxy ).


--- Allan and the Sundered Veil.txt ---

[1] Lloigor L87
Quatermain is taken aback by the two Carters vanishing, but the Time Traveler is not surprised. As he explains to Quatermain, the Time Traveler is the only one whose body is there physically. The others were corporified consciousness, traveling far from where their physical bodies remained, just like Quatermain's body is still in Lady Ragnall's library. When Quatermain also vanishes, the Time Traveler peers into the Aleph and is horrified to see that the adventurer's body has awakened, but is possessed by one of the extracosmic beings. These creatures that are invading reality are identified as the Lloigor , a. k. a. the Great Old Ones . They are the individual components or avatars of an "idea-colony" named Yuggoth : a concept variously identified as a planet, a god, or a state of mind. Despite being aspects of Yuggoth, each Lloigor has its own name and distinct characteristics, as well as an elemental servitor race. The one possessing Quatermain is Ithaqqa , lord of the wind-walkers or Wendigo. Quatermain's disembodied consciousness sees his own body, animated by Ithaqqa, attacking Lady Ragnall and her maid Marisa.

[2] Lloigor L101
When Quatermain also vanishes, the Time Traveler peers into the Aleph and is horrified to see that the adventurer's body has awakened, but is possessed by one of the extracosmic beings. These creatures that are invading reality are identified as the Lloigor , a. k. a. the Great Old Ones . They are the individual components or avatars of an "idea-colony" named Yuggoth : a concept variously identified as a planet, a god, or a state of mind. Despite being aspects of Yuggoth, each Lloigor has its own name and distinct characteristics, as well as an elemental servitor race. The one possessing Quatermain is Ithaqqa , lord of the wind-walkers or Wendigo. Quatermain's disembodied consciousness sees his own body, animated by Ithaqqa, attacking Lady Ragnall and her maid Marisa. He enters his body and fights against the entity. Lady Ragnall dies in shock, but Marisa sees Quatermain's efforts to restrain himself and understands what is happening. She pricks her own hand and uses the blood to draw an ancient talisman while uttering the incantations that she knows can repel the Great Old Ones.

--- Animus.txt ---

[2] Lloigor L53
НАЗВАНИЕ: Animus ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:11:50 СИМВОЛОВ: 1905 Animus Also known as Pwodarauk Lloigor Affiliations Great Old Ones First appearance ADJ : "The Web Planet" Created by Bill Strutton The Animus is an extraterrestrial life form created by the BBC for Doctor Who in 1965. It was later retconned into being a Great Old One during the period when Virgin Books tie-in novels related to Doctor Who were being published in the 1990s. Background The Animus is a Great Old One from the Lloigor race. It was the only one of its race to cross into the physical universe, establishing itself at Vortis and turning the formerly lush planet into an acidic wasteland. It manifested itself within the Carsenome, an organic self-healing palace. The Animus is capable of taking over anything living which came into contact with gold. It resembled a hybrid created by crossing a spider or plant with an octopus. It could generate a fungus-like sustenance which grew into Carsenome, its fortress.

[2] Zhar L51
НАЗВАНИЕ: Anton Zarnak ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:11:53 СИМВОЛОВ: 1773 Anton Zarnak Also known as The Mouthpiece of Zhar Powers and Abilities Magical abilities to an unknown extent Occupation Occult detective Affiliations Zhar and Lloigor (formerly) Species Human First appearance EXP Dead of Night Appearances EXP Perchance to Dream Created by Lin Carter Dr. Anton Zarnak , whose surname is derived from Zhar-Nak, meaning the Mouthpiece of Zhar , is a character created by Mythos author Lin Carter . He is a supernatural detective in the style of others such as William Hope Hodgson Thomas Carnacki Description Zarnak appears to be Eurasian of descent, with a high forehead and jet black hair shot through with a central zigzag of grey. He may be independently wealthy, and lives in a fair degree of luxury with his faithful manservant Ram Singh He appears to possess a degree of magical power, which includes the ability to mesmerise others and to detect whether certain objects have been tampered with.

[3] Zhar and Lloigor L83
He is a supernatural detective in the style of others such as William Hope Hodgson Thomas Carnacki Description Zarnak appears to be Eurasian of descent, with a high forehead and jet black hair shot through with a central zigzag of grey. He may be independently wealthy, and lives in a fair degree of luxury with his faithful manservant Ram Singh He appears to possess a degree of magical power, which includes the ability to mesmerise others and to detect whether certain objects have been tampered with. According to " Dope War of the Black Tong ", Zarnak was once the high priest of Zhar and Lloigor amongst the Tcho-Tcho people on the remote Plateau of Sung , although the temptations offered by the Black Lotus drug resulted in him being ousted from his position. He has multiple doctorates, at least one of which (in metaphysics) was obtained from Miskatonic University , and his expertise has earned him a consultancy position with the Sanbourne Institute of Pacific Antiquities Library Anton Zarnak possesses an extensive collections of occult writings, including: The Black Rituals of Koth-Serapis The Dhôl Chants The Ponape Scripture Appearances Lin Carter's Works Dead of Night " (1988) Perchance to Dream " (1988) Other Works Dope War of the Black Tong " (1996, Robert M.

--- Colin Wilson.txt ---

[2] Lloigor L77
Challenged by August Derleth in the wake of this criticism to show that he could do it better, Wilson produced The Mind Parasites (1967), published by Arkham House , in which humanity is bedeviled by creatures called Tsathogguans . (In a preface, Wilson avers that Lovecraft, "far more than Hemingway or Faulkner, or even Kafka, is a symbol of the outsider-artist in the 20th century".) Wilson then wrote " The Return of the Lloigor ", first published in Tales of the Cthulhu Mythos (1969), which turned Derleth's minor Great Old One Lloigor into an alien species of psychic vampires --a depiction that has influenced subsequent writers, including Robert Anton Wilson, Alan Moore , and Grant Morrison Selected Bibliography Fiction The Mind Parasites (1967) The Philosopher's Stone (1969) (Reprinted, Valancourt Books, 2013) The Return of the Lloigor " (first published 1969 in the anthology Tales of the Cthulhu Mythos ; revised separate edition, Village Press, London, 1974). The Space Vampires (1976) Spider World: The Tower (1987) Spider World: The Delta (1987) Spider World: The Magician (1992) The Tomb of the Old Ones (novella published as half of a double volume alongside a novella by John Grant, 2002) Spider World: Shadowland (2002) Nonfiction The Outsider (1956) The Strength to Dream: Literature and the Imagination (1962) (1971) Order of Assassins (1972)

[2] Geb L895
Conceptions of God in Ancient Egypt Figure 4. Shu separates the sky and the earth (shown in the form of Geb and Nut). Assyrian chief god Assur.

[3] Geb L901
The earth is f but the earth god is Geb, and the sky is pt but the sky goddess is Nut (Figure 4); the sun god Re stands beside jtn, the term for the sun disk, and the moon is called fh, while the two early moon gods are Thoth (Dhwtj, no plausible etymology) and Khons (probably "the wanderer").

[5] Geb L1119
Air and earth are also shared among several deities: Shu as "empty" space, Amun as air in motion or the vivifying breath of wind, and Seth in the destructive might of storms; in addition to Geb, Aker and Tatenen (also called Ptah-Tatenen), at least, there are other gods who em- body the earth.

[8] Geb L1307
O... Osiris, foremost of the westerners . . justified god, lord of endlessness, ruler of eternity, oldest son whom Geb begot, the first one of the womb of Nut, lord of Busiris, ruler of Abydos, sovereign over the land of silence (the realm of the dead), pos- sessor of splendor, great of terror, sacred ram before Naret, divine king, content with Maat, greater than his father, mightier than his mother, lord of what came into being through himself, greatest of the great, chief of his brothers, son of the white crown, whom the red crown bore, lord of lords, ruler of rulers, sovereign, god of OSes 6 These are not mere glorifying phrases for the god who is being worshiped; behind every name and every epithet there is a reality of myth or cult, which is often incorporated more . directly into the invocations by means of wordplay.

[9] Geb L1539
Relief fragments in Turin provide an example of Geb in the early Third Dynasty;" like him, the other cosmic deities are in human form (Figure 4): Nut (the sky), Shu (air), and Atum, the god of the primeval beginning.

[10] Geb L1667
Still other deities have hieroglyphs on their heads: Geb has a goose, Maat an ostrich feather, the goddess of the west the sign for "west," and Nephthys her entire name ("mistress of the house").

[11] Nut L1709
8a: deceased "singer of Amonrasonther." Conceptions of God in Ancient Egypt the sky goddess Nut is depicted nude." Egyptian deities are exempt from the vagaries of fashion.

[13] Geb L2097
The sun god as a child on the primeval lotus. In the system of the Heliopolitan ennead five successive gen- erations of deities can be distinguished: the primeval deity Atum creates the first couple Shu and Tefnut, who are not born in the usual way; their offspring Geb, the god of the earth, and Nut, the goddess of the sky, in turn produce four children— Osiris, Seth, Isis, and Nephthys.

[14] Nut L2097
In the New Kingdom and late period the sky goddess Nut, who bore the sun according to the Pyramid Characteristics of the Gods Texts and the moon according to the Coffin Texts,’ often has the epithet "she who bore the gods."

[15] Nut L2101
This epithet refers to the heavenly bodies, whom the sky goddess daily "bears" and again "swallows"—an idea that leads to the depiction of Nut as a sow.

[16] Nut L2101
* In this case the "gods" are not just the heavenly bodies, as Nut is mother of the stars, but literally all deities.

[17] Geb L2103
Still more widespread than this intensified form is the epithet "father of the gods," which is applied to Atum, Geb, and Shu in addition to the gods just mentioned—that is, to deities who have an essential role in the process of creation."

[35] Nut L3539
But the sky is a number of things—cow, baldachin, water, woman—it is the goddess Nut and the goddess Hathor, and in syncretism a deity a is at the same time another, not-a.

[38] Geb L4703
Geb, earth god of a more universal characier than Aker; Geb is both judge and "hereditary prince" or "father" of the gods, especially of Osiris. Depicted in purely human form (see also Nut).

[39] Nut L4703
Depicted in purely human form (see also Nut).

--- Cthaeghya.txt ---

[2] Cxaxukluth L85
НАЗВАНИЕ: Cthaeghya ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:14:22 СИМВОЛОВ: 3132 Cthaeghya Relatives Cxaxukluth (parent) Cthulhu (brother) Hastur (brother) Ghizguth (brother) Hziulquoigmnzhah (brother) Zstylzhemgni (sister) Klosmiebhyx (sister) Affiliations Great Old Ones First appearance EXP October Surprise Created by Kevin L. O'Brien Cthaeghya is a fictional entity created by Kevin L. O'Brien for his short story " October Surprise ", depicting the character as the sister of Cthulhu and responsible for spawning the Cthulhi Background According to The Book of Merlin , Cthaeghya was birthed by the Outer God known as Cxaxukluth on a black star in the center of a grand nebula, along with her brothers Ghizguth Hzioulquoigmnzhah and Rahb, and their sisters Zstylzhemghi and Klosmiebhyx . Cthaeghya and her family journeyed to Yuggoth , where Rahb mated with the infertile Cthaeghya. They were forced to flee when Cxaxukluth began feeding off its progeny, with Cthaeghya and Rahb fleeing to Xoth . There, Rahb began mating with Idh-yaa , who bore his three sons: Ghatanothoa Ythogtha and Zoth-Ommog . In a fit of rage, Cthaeghya destroyed Idh-yaa and then headed towards Magmhel.

[3] Cxaxukluth L105
O'Brien for his short story " October Surprise ", depicting the character as the sister of Cthulhu and responsible for spawning the Cthulhi Background According to The Book of Merlin , Cthaeghya was birthed by the Outer God known as Cxaxukluth on a black star in the center of a grand nebula, along with her brothers Ghizguth Hzioulquoigmnzhah and Rahb, and their sisters Zstylzhemghi and Klosmiebhyx . Cthaeghya and her family journeyed to Yuggoth , where Rahb mated with the infertile Cthaeghya. They were forced to flee when Cxaxukluth began feeding off its progeny, with Cthaeghya and Rahb fleeing to Xoth . There, Rahb began mating with Idh-yaa , who bore his three sons: Ghatanothoa Ythogtha and Zoth-Ommog . In a fit of rage, Cthaeghya destroyed Idh-yaa and then headed towards Magmhel. During their attack on Xoth, the Elder Beings managed to kidnap Rahb, with his sons escaping to Magmhel in order to tell Cthaeghya about their father's capture.

Klüber, Johann Ludwig Author of Kryptographik (1) . [ HPL Dunwich online text ) 183] K'naa A province or kingdom of ancient Mu . Included Mt. Yaddith-Gho , and ruins left by pre-human races such as the spawn of Yuggoth (that is, the Outer Ones ). Sacrifices were made to Ghatanothoa , whose priesthood was pampered and powerful. Other gods worshipped (and considered more friendly to man) were Shub-Niggurath Nug Yeb , and Yig . At the time of the Year of the Red Moon the nation was ruled by King Thabon . It was then that T'yog made his tragic attempt to free humanity from the tyranny of Ghatanothoa. --- Cthulhu_Files_Store_a091568afc [Cthulhu Files Store_9.txt].txt ---

[1] Yeb L23
H. Barlow Lovecraft's young protegé and literary executor, Barlow was responsible for preserving Lovecraft's manuscripts. He later became a distinguished professor of anthropology. Eyes of the God: Selected Writings of R. H. Barlow (Second Edition, Revised and Expanded) Collects Barlow's early fantasy stories, some written in collaboration with Lovecraft, including many that were long available only in the pages of fan publications from the 1930s. Included is a brief series, "The Annals of the Jinn" that is in the vein of Lovecraft's dreamlands series and Smith's Hyperborean fantasies. There are passing references to some Lovecraftian entities such as Nyarlathotep, Azathoth, Shub-Niggurath, and Yeb, as well as the Smithian Tsathoggua (here called Zotog-Ua). The volume also includes Barlow's poetry and a selection of his nonfiction, including moving essays about Lovecraft and Henry S. Whitehead. The greatly expanded second edition runs to 596 pages. Marcos Legaria, L'Affaire Barlow Bringing Lovecraft's works to a mainstream audience was a journey fraught with complications. In L’Affaire Barlow, the author shows how dangerously close Lovecraft’s work was to being litigated into obscurity. Bruised egos, personal vendettas, and Machiavellian plots abound, making control of the Lovecraft literary estate read like a tale from one of the pulp magazines.

--- Cxaxukluth.txt ---

[1] Cxaxukluth L1
НАЗВАНИЕ: Cxaxukluth ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:15:22 СИМВОЛОВ: 1951 Cxaxukluth Also known as Ksaksa-Kluth, Androgynous Offspring of Azathoth, Illimitable Androgynous Desire Current location Yuggoth , An Unnamed Dimension Relatives Azathoth (parent) Ptmâk (unknown; possibly an avatar or mate) Hziulquoigmnzhah (offspring) Ghisguth (offspring) Tulu (offspring) Tsathoggua (grandchild) Affiliations Outer Gods First appearance CIRCLE The Family Tree of the Gods Appearances EXP : "The Unresponding Gods" Created by Clark Ashton Smith Cxaxukluth Ksaksa-Kluth Illimitable Androgynous Desire ) is an Outer God and is the offspring of Azathoth by spontaneous fission. According to at least one genealogical chart , its progeny are Hziulquoigmnzhah Ghisguth , and Tulu . It is described as an androgynous being, capable of reproducing asexually. Via Ghisguth, it is the grandparent of Tsathoggua Cxaxukluth dwells on Yuggoth .

[2] Ghisguth L89

[4] Nug L105
It is described as an androgynous being, capable of reproducing asexually. Via Ghisguth, it is the grandparent of Tsathoggua Cxaxukluth dwells on Yuggoth . Its immediate family lived with it for a while, but soon left because of its cannibalistic appetites. Nug , the feminine being called Yeb , and then produced their various offspring together. This text also refers to Cxaxukluth as the "Illimitable Androgynous Desire" prior to the split. There is additional speculation that Ptmâk and Cxaxukluth may have only been the Hyperborean names for Nug and Yeb.

[5] Cxaxukluth L111

[9] Ptmâk L131
Cxaxukluth is probably Yeb EXP : "The Second Epistle of Eibon unto his Disciples, or The Apocalypse of Eibon" , Robert M. Price is included in The Book of Eibon (Chaosium, 2002)). Ptmâk has been identified as the immediate parent of Cthulhu (a. k. a. Tulu), which might mean that Ptmâk and Cxaxukluth either mated with each other to spawn Cthulhu, or are merely different names or avatars for the same entity. Behind the Mythos The creature was invented, outside of fiction, in the letters by Clark Ashton Smith to Robert H. Barlow , and published in the article " The Family Tree of the Gods " in The Acolyte fanzine (issue 7, Summer 1944).

Через две недели мы отправились, туда на специально зафрахтованном судне. О нашей цели мы не осмелились прошептать ни слова даже экипажу — из страха, что нас сочтут сумасшедшими и откажутся с нами плыть. Мы были уверены, что наш поиск обернется успехом, что где-то среди еще не нанесенных на карту островов Полинезии мы найдем то, к чему стремимся, и, найдя это, навеки присоединимся к нашим морским братьям, которые служат и ждут дня воскрешения — когда и Ктулху, и Хастур, и Ллойгор, и Йог-Сотот восстанут вновь и одолеют Старших Богов в титанической борьбе, которой не избежать. На Понапе мы устроили себе базу. Иногда мы плавали прямо оттуда; иногда пользовались судном, не обращая внимания на любопытство экипажа. Мы обыскивали все воды, иногда уплывая на несколько дней кряду. Вскоре мое превращение стало полным.

--- Dr. Fo-Lan.txt ---

[1] Lloigor L89
Fo-Lan is incredibly knowledgeable in the matters of the Cthulhu Mythos and has even mastered the art of astral projection, although he refers to it as "telepathy". At the end of the nineteenth century, Fo-Lan was abducted from his home in Peiping by the Tcho-Tcho people , who brought him to their hidden city of Alaozar, in the Burmese Plateau of Sung . Fo-Lan's brother was killed, presumably disfigured to prevent identification, and left in Fo-Lan's place, leading the world to believe that the doctor himself had been murdered. For the next three years, the captive Fo-Lan reluctantly helped the Tcho-Tchos and their leader E-poh to dig deeper into the heart of the Plateau, until they found the chamber where Lloigor was imprisoned. Unbeknownst to his captors, Fo-Lan spent all his spare time trying to use astral projection to contact the Elder Gods in the Orion Belt. When the Tcho-Tchos captured an American explorer named Eric Marsh and brought him to Alaozar, Fo-Lan convinced them to spare the man's life by claiming that Marsh could help them.

[2] Zhar and Lloigor L97
For the next three years, the captive Fo-Lan reluctantly helped the Tcho-Tchos and their leader E-poh to dig deeper into the heart of the Plateau, until they found the chamber where Lloigor was imprisoned. Unbeknownst to his captors, Fo-Lan spent all his spare time trying to use astral projection to contact the Elder Gods in the Orion Belt. When the Tcho-Tchos captured an American explorer named Eric Marsh and brought him to Alaozar, Fo-Lan convinced them to spare the man's life by claiming that Marsh could help them. Fo-Lan told Marsh the story of how the Elder Gods imprisoned Zhar and Lloigor Star-Warriors of Orion descended from the stars to destroy the twin monstrosities and their servants, followed later by the Elder Gods themselves, one of which transported Fo-Lan and Marsh to safety. A Tokyo newspaper later published the news about Fo-Lan being still alive. By the time Eric Marsh wrote his narrative, he told readers that they could seek out Fo-Lan in the village of Bangka, in the province of Shan-si, if they wanted to confirm the veracity of the events narrated. ( AWD The Lair of the Star-Spawn

--- Elder God.txt ---

[1] Zhar and Lloigor L81
These gigantic beings from outer space descended swiftly, circling the Plateau of Sung , and from them great rays of stabbing light shot out toward the hidden fastnesses below. And at the same time, the earth began to tremble. Eric Marsh ( AWD The Lair of the Star-Spawn The Elder Gods , also referred to as the Elder Deities , are fictional extraterrestrial entities featured in the Cthulhu Mythos . They are originally described as looking like huge pillars of light or fire, which dwell in the vicinity of the star Betelgeuse , and are known to protect humans while being inimical to the "evil" gods such as Cthulhu Hastur Zhar and Lloigor AWD The Lair of the Star-Spawn The Horror from the Depths "). In the Expanded Mythos, they originate from another dimension, have mostly human-like appearance and are benevolent in nature. In different parts of the world - such as Egypt, Mesoamerica, and the Mediterranean - they were worshipped by ancient humans. Nowadays, they are remembered as the gods in just about every ancient race, including Greek, Norse, Egyptian, Aztec, and many others.

[Sandwin] Those Who Came in ye shape of Towers of Fire [Elder Gods (1)], return’d whence They had come, and were seen no more, and on all Earth then peace came and was unbrok’n [Lurker] Then the Old Ones, the Elder Gods, returned to the stars of Orion, leaving behind them ever-damned Cthulhu, Lloigor, Zhar, and others. [Lair] . . .

[2] Lloigor L45
[Sandwin] Those Who Came in ye shape of Towers of Fire [Elder Gods (1)], return’d whence They had come, and were seen no more, and on all Earth then peace came and was unbrok’n [Lurker] Then the Old Ones, the Elder Gods, returned to the stars of Orion, leaving behind them ever-damned Cthulhu, Lloigor, Zhar, and others. [Lair] . . . and return’d to that place from whence they had come, Namely, Glyu-Vho, which is among ye stars, and looked upon Earth from ye time when ye leaves fall to that time when ye ploughman becomes habit’d once again to his fields. [Lurker] Glyu-Vho is an alien name for Betelgeuse, the Elder Gods (1) home star in Orion. Lovecraft had suggested the name Glyn-aho to Derleth, who later either altered the spelling or misread Lovecraft's handwriting.

--- Events in H. P. Lovecraft's fiction.txt ---

[3] Nug L1363
Some of occurrences may appear before the known dates below, but will be placed after is no information about the event is established. These unknown dates may themselves be listed out of order. 2169 A. D. Pickman Carter uses strange means to repel the Mongol hordes from Australia. 2518 A. D. Australian physicist Nevil Kingston-Brown, who mindswapped with a Yithian, dies. 5000 A. D. ca Philosopher Yiang-Li from the cruel empire of Tsan-Chan mindswaps with a Yithian. 16,000 A. D. Nug-Soth , a magician of the dark conquerors, mindswaps with a Yithian. 18,000,000 A. D. A half-plastic denizen of the hollow interior of a trans-Plutonian planet mindswaps with a Yithian. Unknown Time Following the final end of humanity, a new race of beetle-like insects arises. These are possessed by the Great Race of Yith, leaving their previous home on a planet orbiting a dark star near Taurus. Eventually, even the beetles die out, and the Yithians send their collective minds into a vegetable species living on Mercury. The first primitive life develops on Venus .


--- FICTION_0e32fc412a.txt ---

[3] Cxaxukluth L71
He flexed his body, testing the strength of the walls of the pocket in which he was trapped, and they fled from him. Azathoth pursued, but the effort split him asunder, releasing fragments some of which he could not recapture. These fragments fed off his Being and grew strong; they became the outer gods. "'One of these was Cxaxukluth, the Androgyne. It fled in fear from Azathoth, and wandered the pocket, eventually coming to a black star in the center of a grand nebula. There it settled and in time birthed many progeny, among the greatest of which were the brothers Ghizguth, Hzioulquoigmnzhah, and Rahb, and the sisters Zstylzhemghi, Klosmiebhyx, and Cthaeghya. In time Cxaxukluth left, taking most of its family with it, and journeyed to Yuggoth, where it remains to this day.

However, Cxaxukluth began feeding off its progeny, so those who could, fled. Ghizguth and Zstylzhemghi hid themselves and Tsathaggua in caverns too deep for Cxaxukluth to reach.

[2] Nut L173
It was a hundred percent bullshit, but it was easier to swallow than the truth, so folks accepted it without question. The only other person who knew the truth was the Sheriff's deputy 'cos he helped burn the house, the barn, and all their contents "to prevent the spread of infection." You can't tell me the neighbors didn't suspect somethin' more though, since a week later they hammered the Circle's stones to powder and dammed the river up so it didn't loop around no more. I never did figure what possessed the Sheriff to get me labelled insane so's I'd be kept in this nut house for the rest of my life. Nor can I see these head shrinkers believin' monsters could beget offspring with a human woman. Even if such were possible, how could they give credence to any tale of a baby that growed to six feet in under a week? It don't make no sense unless they're the ones who's crazy! I sure as hell ain't idiot enough to get myself all but ruined for no doll, but that's what the Sheriff claimed I did!

When Cthugua is set free! When Hastur the Unspeakable is free to fly without restraint! When Yog-Sothoth reveals his true self to all! When Azathoth bubbles over into everything! When Nyarlathotep delivers his final message to the human race!" The grunts and croaks rose to a crescendo. A cacophony of horrific and demonic utterances echoed throughout the evil chamber. Malevolence dripping from every sound. "And finally! When we see the Hounds of Tindalos loosed upon all dimensions! And the likes of Tsathoggua, Ithaqua, Puhipacca, and Lloigor roam freely across the cosmos! When our brethren the Tcho-Tcho People and the abominable snowmen or Mi-Go realize the fruits of their labors! When Dagon is seated upon his rightful throne! And when sunken R'lyeh rises for the last time! To you, oh lord Cthulhu! We give thanks. We give our all!" The Deep Ones were in a frenzy, waving their webbed hands above their heads and croaking and grunting like victorious warriors. Jimmy Swaggart, eat your heart out! The teenage girl on the table was screaming.


--- FICTION_d63f7b864f [Оригинал [Оригинал.txt].txt].txt ---

[1] Zhar L59
In general, my employer was seeking references to a cult or tribe called the "Tcho-Tcho people," rumoured to linger on in certain of the more inaccessible parts of jungled Burma and in Leng -- wherever Leng was supposed to be, for I could not find it in any atlas. They were believed to worship gods or devils with names like "Zhar" and "Lloigor," but so little about them was known for certain, that many authorities seemed to consider them to be merely legendary.

[9] Zhar L203
So I picked a few of them up. There were thousands of them, they wouldn't miss a dozen or so. I thought they might have been a type of currency, but they were too cumbersome -- then a symbol of citizenship? You will have guessed what they were -- the star-stones of the Elder Gods. That was my mistake -- I did not know that they had been placed there to keep Zhar from waking. But something knew I had moved them. They must have some kind of warning device, I don't know what, but something. 'Up from Sarkomand' the book says, 'the Tcho-Tcho people came from the Great Abyss!' Were they there to worship Zhar or to guard him from people like myself?

[10] Zhar L203

And why did they not remove the star-stones, isn't that what the Necrononicon says the servants of the Great Old Ones are supposed to do? I cannot say. Maybe they became decadent over the years and started to worship their prisoner? Who can say?

--- From the Parchments of Pnom.txt ---

[1] Cxaxukluth L101
His main source, albeit not the only one, is an ancient text called the Parchments of Pnom . The time period is unknown, although the scholar mentions a type of whiskey that is "imported from Mars He starts out by revealing that, while Azathoth's androgynous children reproduced asexually like their parent, many of their offspring were single-gendered. For instance, the androgynous Cxaxukluth spawned at least two male sons: Hziulquoigmnzhah and Ghizguth , the latter which married Zstylzhemgni and fathered Tsathoggua. He also mentions a descendant of the gods named Knygathin Zhaum , who regained the Azathothian ability to spawn amorphous children after " the stress of his numerous decapitations ", and hints of another legend that he hasn't translated yet. For details concerning a deity named Tulu or Cthulhu , the author recommends that the correspondent contact a certain Ech-Pi-El , who can provide more information.

[2] Cxaxukluth L137
He also mentions a descendant of the gods named Knygathin Zhaum , who regained the Azathothian ability to spawn amorphous children after " the stress of his numerous decapitations ", and hints of another legend that he hasn't translated yet. For details concerning a deity named Tulu or Cthulhu , the author recommends that the correspondent contact a certain Ech-Pi-El , who can provide more information. Although Cxaxukluth's sons were born far away from the Solar System , the entire family eventually came to reside on Yuggoth , where Cxaxukluth remains to this day. To escape his parent's cannibalistic inclinations, Hziulquoigmnzhah moved on to Yaksh , where the natives worshiped him, then onto Cykranosh , followed aeons later by his nephew Tsathoggua. The encounter of Eibon with Hzioulquoigmnzhah many ages later is mentioned.

[3] Ptmâk L205
The author speculates that the natives of with and K'n-yan might have misunderstood or forgotten parts of the legend, thus explaining any potential contradictions told in the narrative of Zamacona Moving on to other questions, the scholar reveals that Tsathoggua's wife was known by names such as Chushax or Zishaik , and that their child, Zvilpogghua , is "more male than anything else". Cthulhu and his kin are the children of an entity named Ptmâk . The term "child of Nug" is used, but it's not clear whether it refers to Ptmâk or is another name for Cthulhu's species (the latter which might imply that Ptmâk is in fact Nug ). The goddess Yhoundeh was spawned by the androgynous Zyhumé , which dwells in the cavern of the Archetypes that " was visited by the ill-starred Ralibar Vooz ".

[4] Nug L207
The author speculates that the natives of with and K'n-yan might have misunderstood or forgotten parts of the legend, thus explaining any potential contradictions told in the narrative of Zamacona Moving on to other questions, the scholar reveals that Tsathoggua's wife was known by names such as Chushax or Zishaik , and that their child, Zvilpogghua , is "more male than anything else". Cthulhu and his kin are the children of an entity named Ptmâk . The term "child of Nug" is used, but it's not clear whether it refers to Ptmâk or is another name for Cthulhu's species (the latter which might imply that Ptmâk is in fact Nug ). The goddess Yhoundeh was spawned by the androgynous Zyhumé , which dwells in the cavern of the Archetypes that " was visited by the ill-starred Ralibar Vooz ". There is, in the author's opinion, strong evidence that Yhoundeh married Nyarlathotep , who is described as "the Azathothian flute-player".

--- Garoth_(Cthulhu_Universalis)_d1e098ff27.txt ---

[1] Yeb L45
Garoth was generally polite and often experienced sympathy for victims and disapproval of those doing violent things. Still, his standards seemed to slip when it was expedient: "Garoth supposed he would have to sacrifice her. . . well, no matter." It was not Garoth's custom to shilly-shally. During his random adventures, Garoth Observed a tragic romance between a milk-maid and a faun. Observed the harsh punishment of a beautiful but lethal female creature. Attempted to rescue an imprisoned maiden, only to repent when he discovered that she was monstrous. Witnessed a cult worshipping Yeb. Garoth considered himself more powerful than Yeb's worshippers, but mightily feared Yeb himself and fled in haste when Yeb arrived with Yog-Sototh and the bitch-goddess Shub-Niggarauth. 1. [RHB Temple] 2. [RHB Garoth] 3. [RHB Misfortunes]

--- Ghizguth.txt ---

[1] Ghisguth L15
НАЗВАНИЕ: Ghizguth ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:18:34 СИМВОЛОВ: 928 Ghizguth Also known as The Sound in the Deep Waters Ghisguth Ghisghuth Relatives Cxaxukluth (father) Hziulquoigmnzhah (sibling) Tsathoggua (offspring) Mate Zstylzhemghi Species Great Old Ones First appearance CIRCLE Selected Letters of Clark Ashton Smith Appearances EXP Malleus Monstrorum Created by Clark Ashton Smith Ghizguth The Sound in the Deep Waters ) is a fictional entity in the Cthulhu Mythos . The character first appeared in Clark Ashton Smith family tree of Azathoth ("Genealogical Chart of the Elder Gods"). Ghizguth was later incorporated into the role-playing game Call of Cthulhu EXP Malleus Monstrorum Overview Ghizguth is one of the Great Old Ones . According to the Parchments of Pnom Pnom being the chief Hyperborean genealogist as well as a noted prophet), he is a male spawn of Cxaxukluth and the brother of Hziulquoigmnzhah and Tulu . He is also the mate of Zstylzhemghi and the father of Tsathoggua

[2] Cxaxukluth L97

--- Gnoph-keh.txt ---

[1] Zhar L77
P. Lovecraft and Hazel Heald For the human Hyperborea species, see Gnophkeh Gnoph-keh , also known as Gnoph-Hek are sharp-horned, coarse-haired beings with six long-limbs, found in Greenland and the Arctic Circle. Empowered with blizzard summoning and reducing temperatures. Connected with Rhan-Tegoth (whom they seem to worship) and possibly Ithaqua Zhar and Lloigor (god). In early texts (such as the Book of Eibon ) it is a race of vicious cannibals who were driven from Lomar by mankind and the Voormis , but in later texts it appears as a lone creature, sometimes called "the Hairy Thing" and is confused with Rhan-Tegoth. Some texts call them "reptilian" although hairy reptiles are far from common. It is now on the brink of extinction and only encountered in ones. The Gnophkeh are either creatures of air or water.


--- Gods and Myths of Northern Europe (H.R. Ellis Davidson) (z-library.sk, 1lib.sk, z-lib.sk).txt ---

[14] Nut L6313
Apples and nuts from the land of promise, renewing youth and freeing those who ate them from the tyranny of time, are a familiar feature in Irish sagas, and one that goes back to an early date. It may be noted that on one occasion Loki turned Idun and her apples into a nut, and escaped with it in his claws as he flew in the form of a bird (see p. 39 ). Here then we have the association between eternal youth and nuts and apples which is found in the Irish stories. Conceivably however the association of fruit, and particularly the apple, with the gods was already present in Germanic heathenism.

Likewise, the so-called "Elder Gods" in this franchise are the most active Great Old Ones in the universe, often playing cosmic games among themselves. In the League of Extraordinary Gentlemen universe, the Great Old Ones (collectively known as the Lloigor) are the individual components or manifestations of an "idea-colony" known as Yuggoth: a metaphysical hive-mind that also functions both as a place and a deity. Despite being part of a greater whole, each of these manifestations has a name, distinct attributes, and an elemental species that serves them.

--- Greek Religion (Walter Burkert) (z-library.sk, 1lib.sk, z-lib.sk).txt ---

[9] Seb L12775
Yet in the later language this word has fallen into disuse, while the word newly formed from the same root, deisidaimon fearing daimons, is used almost exclusively in a pejorative sense to mean superstition, that which seems ridiculous. Have the Greeks ‘forgotten the experience of the creeps’? To designate the awe that spreads from the gods the root seb- appears; etymologically it too points back to danger and flight, yet in Greek, reverence and admiration come to the fore: ‘ sebas holds me as I behold’; a moderate mysterium tremendum is transferred into augustum. Sebesthai is close to aideisthai and almost coincides with hazesthai . Gods and everything that belongs to them, festivals, temples, sacrifices, are semna , revered, grand, and august, and so too are clothes, manner of speech, and behaviour at the festivals of the gods.

--- H. P. Lovecraft and Hazel Heald, Out of the Aeons [Оригинал.txt].txt ---

[4] Nug L145
T’yog had thought long on the powers of the various gods, and had had strange dreams and revelations touching the life of this and earlier worlds. In the end he felt sure that the gods friendly to man could be arrayed against the hostile gods, and believed that Shub-Niggurath, Nug, and Yeb, as well as Yig the Serpent-god, were ready to take sides with man against the tyranny and presumption of Ghatanothoa. Inspired by the Mother Goddess, T’yog wrote down a strange formula in the hieratic Naacal of his order, which he believed would keep the possessor immune from the Dark God’s petrifying power.


--- H. P. Lovecraft and Zealia Bishop, [Оригинал.txt].txt ---

[9] Nug L343
From the whole mass of gold and stone a monotonous roar of activity droned outward over the plain, while cavalcades and streams of wagons were constantly entering and leaving over the great gold- or stone-paved roads. Several times Gll’-Hthaa-Ynn paused to shew Zamacona some particular object of interest, especially the temples of Yig, Tulu, Nug, Yeb, and the Not-to-Be-Named One which lined the road at infrequent intervals, each in its embowering grove according to the custom of K’n-yan. These temples, unlike those of the deserted plain beyond the mountains, were still in active use; large parties of mounted worshippers coming and going in constant streams. Gll’-Hthaa-Ynn took Zamacona into each of them, and the Spaniard watched the subtle orgiastic rites with fascination and repulsion.

[10] Nug and Yeb L343
These temples, unlike those of the deserted plain beyond the mountains, were still in active use; large parties of mounted worshippers coming and going in constant streams. Gll’-Hthaa-Ynn took Zamacona into each of them, and the Spaniard watched the subtle orgiastic rites with fascination and repulsion. The ceremonies of Nug and Yeb sickened him especially—so much, indeed, that he refrained from describing them in his manuscript. One squat, black temple of Tsathoggua was encountered, but it had been turned into a shrine of Shub-Niggurath, the All-Mother and wife of the Not-to-Be-Named One. This deity was a kind of sophisticated Astarte, and her worship struck the pious Catholic as supremely obnoxious.

[1] Lloigor L105
Luhy Distone is a Cthulhi , who was a former employee at the Cthulhu Corp. gaming and entertainment company. R'lyeh is a theme park that has intergalactic contraband and human trafficking. Nodens is one of the space smugglers there, who uses Night-gaunts to kidnap his victims. Dagon is the R'lyeh express water taxi. Tamao Kurei (a human friend of Nyaruko) gets possessed by a Yithian named Isuka. Ghatanothoa appears in episode 11 and 12 of the TV series, taking the form of a little girl named Ghutatan. She is accompanied by Roy Fogger, who is later revealed to be an evil Lloigor that seeks to steal all of Earth's eroge games. A gang of Shoggoths also try to attack Ghutatan. Ut'Ulls-Hr'Her appears as a yaoi fangirl, under the name Tsuruko. Zhar and Lloigor run a sketchy environmentalist group that receives their funds by creating new endangered species to protect. Boss Mi-Go, leader of the Mi-Go race, appears as a one-shot antagonist. Another member of Kuuko's species is named Clark Ashton Smith Members of the Space Defense Agency's Dreamlands Management Division include Bast and N'tse-Kaambl Atoko Shirogane, a character based on Atlach-Nacha , is introduced later in the series.

НАЗВАНИЕ: Hziulquoigmnzhah ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:21:12 СИМВОЛОВ: 1121 Hziulquoigmnzhah Also known as The God of Cykranosh Ziulquag-Manzah Origin Unknown, possibly Xoth Current location Cykranosh (Saturn) Relatives Cxaxukluth (father) Ghisguth (brother) Tsathoggua (nephew) Scathach (daughter) Ysbaddaden (son) Mate Klosmiebhyx First appearance CIRCLE The Door to Saturn Created by Clark Ashton Smith Iqhui dlosh odhqlonqh ["Be on your way"] Hziulquoigmnzhah's words to Eibon CIRCLE The Door to Saturn Hziulquoigmnzhah (also known as Ziulquaz-Manzah ) is a fictional entity created by Clark Ashton Smith for his short story " The Door to Saturn Overview Hziulquoigmnzhah is described as having "ludicrously short legs ... exceedingly elongated arms, and [a] round, sleepy-looking head that was pendulous from a spherical body". It is slow-moving, and shows no real interest in either helping or hindering the sorcerer Eibon or his pursuer, the zealot Morghi. --- Independent race.txt ---

[1] Lloigor L55
НАЗВАНИЕ: Independent race ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:21:38 СИМВОЛОВ: 1945 Independent races are one of the three categories employed to classify species in the role-playing game Call of Cthulhu , along with servitor races and fabulous creatures . Unlike servitor races, independent races are not intrinsically associated with one particular deity . While some might be irreligious, others might still form cults. The game further classifies them as "Greater Independent Races" or "Lesser Independent Races", depending on the danger posed by an average individual of the species. Greater Independent Races Bunyips Chthonians Colours out of Space Dholes Flying Polyps Gnoph-keh Great Race of Yith Great Trees Horses of the Invisible Hounds of Tindalos Larvae of the Other Gods Lloigor Lords of Tindalos Shoggoth Lords Spawn of the Green Abyss Tatsu Tengu Zy'tl Q'ae The following individual beings are also classified as Greater Independents: Greater Independent Species Beast in the Pit unique being Gazira unique being Keeper of Dreams unique being Mr.

--- Ithaqua.txt ---

[1] Zhar and Lloigor L45
НАЗВАНИЕ: Ithaqua ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:21:50 СИМВОЛОВ: 4242 Ithaqua Also known as Death-Walker God of the Cold God of the Winds Ithaka Lord of the Winds Wendigo The White Silence Wind-Walker Yeti Avaloth (possibly) Relatives Shub-Niggurath (mother) Hastur (father) Zhar and Lloigor (siblings) Offspring Armandra (daughter) Affiliations Great Old Ones First appearance AWD The Thing That Walked on the Wind Appearances AWD Ithaqua Beyond the Threshold EXP The Burrowers Beneath The Transition of Titus Crow Born of the Winds Jendick's Swamp Wrath of the Wind-Walker Allan and the Sundered Veil Created by August Derleth Ithaqua , also known as the Wind-Walker or the Wendigo , is a fictional entity in the Cthulhu Mythos . The titular creature debuted in August Derleth 's short story " The Thing That Walked on the Wind ", which was inspired by Algernon Blackwood 's 1910 tale The Wendigo Background Ithaqua is one of the Great Old Ones Wendigo and possibly the Yeti Ithaqua's cult is small, but he is greatly feared in the far north. Fearful denizens of Siberia, Canada , and Alaska often leave sacrifices for Ithaqua; not as worship but as appeasement. Those who join his cult are granted immunity to extremely cold temperatures.

[2] Zhar and Lloigor L157
The titular creature debuted in August Derleth 's short story " The Thing That Walked on the Wind ", which was inspired by Algernon Blackwood 's 1910 tale The Wendigo Background Ithaqua is one of the Great Old Ones Wendigo and possibly the Yeti Ithaqua's cult is small, but he is greatly feared in the far north. Fearful denizens of Siberia, Canada , and Alaska often leave sacrifices for Ithaqua; not as worship but as appeasement. Those who join his cult are granted immunity to extremely cold temperatures. He often uses Shantaks , a colossal avian "lesser race", as servitors Derleth classified Ithaqua as an elemental of air, alongside Hastur Zhar and Lloigor .

[3] Zhar L175
Fearful denizens of Siberia, Canada , and Alaska often leave sacrifices for Ithaqua; not as worship but as appeasement. Those who join his cult are granted immunity to extremely cold temperatures. He often uses Shantaks , a colossal avian "lesser race", as servitors Derleth classified Ithaqua as an elemental of air, alongside Hastur Zhar and Lloigor . However, in at least one of the many conflicts among the Great Old Ones, Ithaqua seems to have sided with Cthulhu against Hastur and Cthugha AWD The Black Island Friedrich von Junzt Unaussprechlichen Kulten claims that Ithaqua, Zhar, and Lloigor are the children of Hastur and Shub-Niggurath EXP The Horror in the Gallery Ithaqua might be the same entity as Avaloth , and possibly Aphoom-Zhah EXP Wrath of the Wind-Walker "), although the latter is generally regarded as a child of Cthugha ( EXP The Acolyte of the Flame Ithaqua figures prominently in Brian Lumley Titus Crow Mythos series, ruling the ice-world of Borea. In Lumley's works, Ithaqua periodically treads the winds of space between Earth and Borea, bringing helpless victims back to Borea to worship him among its snowy wastes. He frequently attempts to reproduce with humanoid females, hoping to create offspring which can surpass his own limitations, imposed by the Elder Gods , and so help free the rest of the Great Old Ones. It is suggested that Ithaqua has the ulterior motive of desiring offspring to assuage his bitter loneliness, as he is the only one of his kind.


--- Ithaqua_(Cthulhu_Universalis)_08abd65f96 [Ithaqua (Cthulhu Universalis).txt].txt ---

[3] Lloigor L81
Leaders, Rivals, and Allies David, a librarian at Miskatonic, said that Hastur, Ithaqua, and Lloigor lead the forces of air. But Ithaqua seems a lesser being than Hastur: for Henry Lucas prayed for Ithaqua to take his body to the feet of that greater being, Hastur. There is a feud between Hastur and Cthugha on the one hand, and Cthulhu and Ithaqua on the other. Some of the Ancient Ones have devotees in common with Ithaqua. The beings at Sandwin house chanted to Ithaqua along with Lloigor, Shub-Niggurath, and Cthulhu ; and the voices outside Josiah Alwyn's house chanted to Ithaqua along with Cthulhu and Shub-Niggurath. Voices heard in the Wood of N'gai indicated that Ithaqua shall serve the "Father of the million favored ones"; that is, Nyarlathotep. The Great Race fled to the future to escape Ithaqua ("That which Walketh on ye Winds") and the Elder Things (1) ("ye winds & ye Voices"), and will do so again. From this, it appears that the Elder Things are allies or servants of Ithaqua.

[4] Lloigor L169
However, Lucas spoke as if it were a punishment he had incurred. One of Ithaqua's traveling companions was apparently not a victim. Asa Sandwin said that he had ridden with Ithaqua high over the face of the earth, over Egypt and Samarkand, Hawaii and the Pacific, and the great white silences. Sandwin said that he was not afraid of Ithaqua, and could foil him. Eventually Sandwin fell victim to a different air elemental, Lloigor. Appearance Ithaqua is the one god of whom no totems bear sign. However, Josiah Alwyn's body was found with a stone miniature of a monstrosity walking on the winds above the earth. At night, Ithaqua may appear simply as a huge dark outline against the stars, a horrible caricature of man. He is similarly described as a giant man-shaped shadow with two starlike eyes.


--- K'n-yanian.txt ---

[1] Nug and Yeb L105
Similarly the society and culture of the K'n-yanians has collapsed into barbaric splendor, with bloodsport and other similar entertainments replacing their previous intellectual pursuits. The natives of K'n-yan are immortal (except by violence or suicide), and have fallen into decline by losing interest in having all their needs met. To survive the boredom, they indulged in grotesque entertainments and the worship of sinister gods they had discovered while exploring the abyss of N'kai (where they had encounters with the Formless Spawn of Tsathoggua ). The natives worshiped Azathoth Nyarlathotep Ghatanothoa Nug and Yeb and Shub-Niggurath , as well as Han the Dark. Their titular gods, to whom they dedicate rich shrines and statues, are Yig and Cthulhu , whom they call "Tulu." According to them, Tulu brought with him an alien metal, which they use as currency after they extracted it from ancient cyclopean idols created on other worlds. This metal was shown to be the only substance that could harm or ward off the K'n-Yanians while they were intangible. Tulu also taught them to write in the R'lyeh glyph form, which has evolved over time.

--- Kishin Houkou Demonbane (OVA).txt ---

[1] Zhar and Lloigor L57
It was accompanied with the limited DX and KADOKAWA THE Best version of the eponymous visual novel , taking place at some point during the main story. Synopsis After arriving in Arkham City, aspiring photojournalist Lilli M. Bridge got a job at the Daily Arkham, a newspaper publisher that was looking for pictures of the giant robot Demonbane to include in their articles. Lilli tries to interview several of the residents including Leica Crusade, Lieutenant Stone and Daijuuji Kurou, but has little to no success at getting any information. When she encounters Al Azif in an area that was restricted by the Hadou Financial Group, Lilli discovers a mysterious piece of alloy as well as successfully photographing Al's missing fragments, known as Zhar and Lloigor . Lilli was nearly attacked by the fragments before being rescued by Kurou. Back at the Daily Arkham, Lilli's boss had no interest in Zhar and Lloigor, referring to it as an "insignificant occult incident" and scolds her for not getting photos of Demonbane instead. However, the alloy that she retrieved was speculated to be created by the Hadou Financial Group, leading to Lilli coming to the conclusion that the organization must have some connection to Demonbane.

[2] Zhar and Lloigor L61
Lilli tries to interview several of the residents including Leica Crusade, Lieutenant Stone and Daijuuji Kurou, but has little to no success at getting any information. When she encounters Al Azif in an area that was restricted by the Hadou Financial Group, Lilli discovers a mysterious piece of alloy as well as successfully photographing Al's missing fragments, known as Zhar and Lloigor . Lilli was nearly attacked by the fragments before being rescued by Kurou. Back at the Daily Arkham, Lilli's boss had no interest in Zhar and Lloigor, referring to it as an "insignificant occult incident" and scolds her for not getting photos of Demonbane instead. However, the alloy that she retrieved was speculated to be created by the Hadou Financial Group, leading to Lilli coming to the conclusion that the organization must have some connection to Demonbane. Partnered with Kurou, Lilli fails to gain more information by questioning the group leader Hadou Ruri, with the Daily Arkham also encouraging her to not pursue any articles involving their organization's activity. Depressed and disappointed, Lilli got drunk while hanging out with Kurou, who warned her to not get any closer to the "dark realm".

[3] Zhar and Lloigor L67
Partnered with Kurou, Lilli fails to gain more information by questioning the group leader Hadou Ruri, with the Daily Arkham also encouraging her to not pursue any articles involving their organization's activity. Depressed and disappointed, Lilli got drunk while hanging out with Kurou, who warned her to not get any closer to the "dark realm". Ignoring his warnings, Lilli decides to visit the restricted area again, where she would accidentally get caught in one of Doctor West 's traps. Although the trap was actually meant for Zhar and Lloigor, West set his sights on using Lilli for human experiments but was interrupted by Kurou, knocking West out of the way and freeing Lilli. Shortly afterwards, Al arrives to assist Kurou and retrieve her missing fragments before they get into a confrontation with a Destroyer Robot piloted by West and his android girl Elsa. While piloting Demonbane, Kurou and Al defeated the Destroyer Robot by chopping its limbs off with Zhar and Lloigor, before it was completely obliterated by their finishing move Lemuria Impact. Mythos Connections Lilli M. Bridge's name is derived from Edwin M. Lillibrige One of the employees at the Daily Arkham was named Blake, a reference to Robert Blake


--- Knygathin Zhaum.txt ---

[1] Cxaxukluth L109
The child of Sfatlicllp and a Voormi , Knygathin Zhaum was a reaver and cannibal. Upon its capture by the constabulary of Commoriom , headsman Athammaus attempted to execute it by beheading, but because of its preternatural heritage, such attempts proved unsuccessful and only served to temporarily inconvenience the creature. As the folks of Commoriom realised that they stood no chance against this seemingly-unkillable entity, they left the fabulous city to its fate and re-established the capital at Uzuldaroum. As a descendant of Cxaxukluth , Knygathin Zhaum reproduced by fission and thus created an Azathothian strain among the Hyperborean Voormi, with which it later repopulated Hyperborea after humankind deserted the capital city of Commoriom.

--- Lloigor (Cthulhu Universalis).txt ---

[4] Lloigor L39
Of the Great Old Ones Lloigor, Zhar, and the other Lords of Air are among the Ancient Ones (2) or Great Old Ones (3) who rebelled against the Elder Gods (1) ][ ][ . The rebellion failed and the Elder Gods cast them out.

[5] Lloigor L59
The rebellion failed and the Elder Gods cast them out. Their Resting Place When the rebellious Great Old Ones (3) were defeated, Lloigor and Zhar were buried alive in deep and unknown caverns beneath the Lake of Dread and the surrounding Plateau of Sung , in Burma (now Myanmar) .

[8] Lloigor L93
Servants and Worshippers The various Great Old Ones, such as Lloigor, have their own bodies of worshippers.

[13] Lloigor L131
Lloigor's Victims Lloigor can draw the body from the earth piecemeal, and leaves the victim's empty clothes behind, as if the body were drawn or sucked out of them.

[14] Lloigor L143
Asa Sandwin was pledged by his father to to serve the spawn of Cthulhu and Lloigor among the Tcho-Tcho people in remote Tibet, to serve them in after-life in their constant struggle against the domination of the Elder Gods.

[23] Lloigor L187
Sylvan Phillips' notes mentioned Lloigor. Appearance Lloigor is described as "a living mass of shuddering horror, a ghastly mountain of sensate, quivering flesh, whose tentacles, far-flung in the dim reaches of the subterranean cavern, emitted a strange humming sound, while from the depths of the creature's body [come] a weird and horrific ululation." Lloigor's eyes gleam greenly.

[30] Lloigor L233
Lloigor's twin Zhar is also associated with Arcturus, for worshippers of Nyarlathotep chanted that "Zhar shall be summoned from Arcturus, by the command of ’Umr at-Tawil, Guardian of the Gate."

--- Many-Angled One.txt ---

[1] Lloigor L15
НАЗВАНИЕ: Many-Angled One ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:24:20 СИМВОЛОВ: 4382 Many-Angled One Sociocultural Characteristics Also known as Lloigor Old Ones Elder Gods Chthonic Deities Chthonic Entities Nameless Ones Undying Ones Great Old Ones Outsiders Origin Unknown Real World Information First appearance ADJ 2000 AD Vol 1 536 Appearances EXP The Thanos Imperative The Laundry Files Created by Grant Morrison The Many-Angled Ones are a race of extradimensional entities from the expanded Cthulhu Mythos . The term "Many-Angled One" was originally created by Grant Morrison for Zenith , a series published in the science fiction comic magazine 2000 AD by Fleetway Publications. It would later be used for several other works, most notably in Marvel Comics Zenith The Many-Angled Ones (also known as the Lloigor ) exist in a space that contains more dimensions than our universe therefore they appear to humans as "many angled."

[2] Lloigor L87
The term "Many-Angled One" was originally created by Grant Morrison for Zenith , a series published in the science fiction comic magazine 2000 AD by Fleetway Publications. It would later be used for several other works, most notably in Marvel Comics Zenith The Many-Angled Ones (also known as the Lloigor ) exist in a space that contains more dimensions than our universe therefore they appear to humans as "many angled." Therefore when one of them manifests in our universe, it appears to us as the floating disconnected body parts of a larger creature. Unlike most Lovecraftian entities, that often seem to have unknown albeit hostile motivations, the goal of the Many-Angled Ones is to impose on the multiverse a rigid geometrical order thereby making everything run like clockwork. No matter what host body a Many-Angled One takes, it possesses superhuman strength, reflexes, and speed. It's extremely durable and nearly invulnerable. From the eyes it incredibly destructive beams.

--- Muvians.txt ---

[1] Nug and Yeb L79
They were based on the pseudoscientific writings of Augustus Le Plongeon and James Churchward , who equated Mu with Atlantis and Lemuria , respectively. In the Cthulhu Mythos , these three continents are portrayed as distinct nations that coexisted and traded with each other during the same precataclysmic time period ( CIRCLE The Shadow Kingdom HPL Bothon CIRCLE The Warder of Knowledge The Muvians were first incorporated into the Mythos by Hazel Heald and H. P. Lovecraft in their short story " Out of the Aeons In the Mythos Religion The people of Mu worshiped many different deities , including Ghatanothoa Shub-Niggurath Yig Nug and Yeb HPL Out of the Aeons Cthulhu Iod Vorvadoss CIRCLE The Invaders "), and Ythogtha K'naa EXP The Thing in the Pit Every year, the priests of Ghatanothoa sacrificed 12 young warriors and 12 young maidens to their god on the flaming altars of a marble temple at the base of Mt. Yaddith-Gho . The people of Mu believed that, without these sacrifices to appease it, Ghatanothoa would emerge from the mountain and cast its petrifying gaze on all below.

--- Nug-Soth (alphabet).txt ---

[3] Nug L91
One can infer such a thing, but such a statement or inference is not present in the 1978 work. In this sense, Wilson and Hay are really using Mythos names as labels for disparate and in many cases arguably unrelated material. The Nug-Soth letters are based on the Coelbren inscriptions in Coelbren, Glamorganshire, Wales , and on the allegedly forged alphabet alleged to have been created rather than discovered by Iolo Morganwg, and first described by Iolo Morganwg's son Taliesin ab Iolo in works published in 1840. The Dark Age inscriptions from the sixth century AD and earlier are matters of historical fact. Using the Coelbren Script, in the form in which it appears on the Dark Age stonework and graves rather than the simplified form from Morganwg, implies several interesting links to the historical King Arthur, ley lines and the Lloigor and was undoubtedly intended to do so given the bent of Wilson's other contributions to the Mythos.

[4] Lloigor L101
The Nug-Soth letters are based on the Coelbren inscriptions in Coelbren, Glamorganshire, Wales , and on the allegedly forged alphabet alleged to have been created rather than discovered by Iolo Morganwg, and first described by Iolo Morganwg's son Taliesin ab Iolo in works published in 1840. The Dark Age inscriptions from the sixth century AD and earlier are matters of historical fact. Using the Coelbren Script, in the form in which it appears on the Dark Age stonework and graves rather than the simplified form from Morganwg, implies several interesting links to the historical King Arthur, ley lines and the Lloigor and was undoubtedly intended to do so given the bent of Wilson's other contributions to the Mythos.

--- Plateau of Sung.txt ---

[1] Lloigor L43
НАЗВАНИЕ: Plateau of Sung ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:27:03 СИМВОЛОВ: 624 Not to be confused with the Plateau of Tsang Plateau of Sung Location Burma First appearance AWD The Lair of the Star-Spawn Created by August Derleth and Mark Schorer The Plateau of Sung is a plateau in Burma on which the Tcho-Tcho People are centered. The city of Alaozar stood on the Isle of Stars in the Lake of Dread, hidden from common view by a barrier of trees (if they were trees). The gods Lloigor and Zhar are situated here. Sometimes called the Sung Region, the area is shunned by the non-Tcho-Tcho natives, and few men have penetrated the jungles to approach it. It is sometimes confused with the Plateau of Leng

--- Ptmâk.txt ---

[3] Ptmâk L39
НАЗВАНИЕ: Ptmâk ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:27:10 СИМВОЛОВ: 790 Ptmâk Relatives Cthulhu (immediate descendant) First appearance September 10th, 1934 letter to R. H. Barlow Created by Clark Ashton Smith Ptmâk Cthulhu (Kthulhut) and his race (the Cosmic Octopi ). It appears to have remained dwelling of Vhoorl and is thus quite obscure. Ptmâk has some connection to Nug , and may be another name for Nug. Behind the Mythos Ptmâk was created by Clark Ashton Smith in letters to R. H. Barlow Lin Carter attempted to incorporate some of the information into his work. The letter is written ambiguously about who is a child of Nug: "The immediate parent of Cthulhu and his race (child of Nug) was Ptmâk".

[4] Ptmâk L47

[5] Nug L59
Ptmâk has some connection to Nug , and may be another name for Nug. Behind the Mythos Ptmâk was created by Clark Ashton Smith in letters to R. H. Barlow Lin Carter attempted to incorporate some of the information into his work. The letter is written ambiguously about who is a child of Nug: "The immediate parent of Cthulhu and his race (child of Nug) was Ptmâk". Comparing Smith's letter with his and Lovecraft's Family trees of Azathoth suggests that Ptmâk and Cxaxukluth are the Hyperborean versions of Nug and Yeb.

[6] Ptmâk L59

The letter is written ambiguously about who is a child of Nug: "The immediate parent of Cthulhu and his race (child of Nug) was Ptmâk". Comparing Smith's letter with his and Lovecraft's Family trees of Azathoth suggests that Ptmâk and Cxaxukluth are the Hyperborean versions of Nug and Yeb.


--- Red Americans.txt ---

[1] Zhar L67
2: The Horrors That Are & Shall Be by 01Publishing in October 2015. Synopsis The plant supervisor of a hellish Chinese work camp which uses Tcho-Tcho labour to assemble a revolutionary new toy watches in disgust as her labour force are physically changed by the bizarre material used in their construction. After being introduced to a Tcho-Tcho priestess, she makes a deal with a dark god - either Zhar or Lloigor , the story is unclear - to spread their malevolent influence.


--- Red Star, Yellow Sign.txt ---

[2] Lloigor L65
He soon uncovers a plot by mystical forces to interfere in the affairs of humankind and, after discovering the entity controlling the human assassin - a monstrous Lloigor - embarks on a course of action which he believes will result in the purging of all supernatural elements from his country. The story is interspersed with a series of missives between R'lyeh and the Leningrad Operational Center. These reveal that the initial assassination attempt was the result of poorly-planned meddling in human affairs; the final one explains how the manipulation of Yezhov and the sacrifice of a Lloigor agent had resulted in a violent program which would keep the secrets of the supernatural puppet masters safe for centuries. Behind the Mythos During the course of his investigations, Yezhov discovers a small statue in the apartment of the assassin which he describes as looking like " a bat-winged man with staring eyes and a beard of squidlike tentacles ." This is a clear nod to the statuette recovered during Inspector Legrasse 's 1907 raid of the Cthulhu Cult , and ties in with the R'lyeh communications which exposit the antagonist's motivations in "Red Star, Yellow Sign."

[3] Lloigor L83
Behind the Mythos During the course of his investigations, Yezhov discovers a small statue in the apartment of the assassin which he describes as looking like " a bat-winged man with staring eyes and a beard of squidlike tentacles ." This is a clear nod to the statuette recovered during Inspector Legrasse 's 1907 raid of the Cthulhu Cult , and ties in with the R'lyeh communications which exposit the antagonist's motivations in "Red Star, Yellow Sign." Although the entity encountered by Yezhov is named as a Lloigor, it bears no resemblance to the more commonly depicted members of that species, being described as having "vast eyes like glowing saucers, surrounded by a mass of squirming tentacles patterned in colours that shifted and shimmered," a much more generic Cthulhu Mythos description than its reptilian namesake. The Yellow Sign mentioned in the title refered to symbols etched on the statue found by Yezhov and imprinted on sewer walls by the Lloigor. Usually associated with the Great Old One Hastur , in this story the Sign was tied to Hastur's half-brother Cthulhu , the two entities being more commonly depicted as antagonistic in later Mythos stories. Trivia The assassination of Sergei Kirov was a real-world event which occurred in the Soviet Union in December 1934.

--- Servitor race.txt ---

[4] Zhar and Lloigor L253
As for their origins, many servitor races are believed to have been created, spawned, or sired by their masters. For instance, the Tcho-Tcho people are said to have been born from "seeds" left by Zhar and Lloigor, and have since been trying to free their progenitors from the subterranean chambers where the Elder Gods have trapped them ( AWD The Lair of the Star-Spawn "). The Miri Nigri were given life by Chaugnar Faugn , who made them from the flesh of Earth animals. Unlike most other races, the Miri Nigri have no mind of their own, and live only to serve their creator.

[5] Nug L317
Conversely, the Brothers of Chaugnar Faugn are more accurately described as dimensional extensions of Chaugnar, as opposed to individual beings ( CIRCLE The Horror from the Hills ). Similarly, the Fire Vampires are merely extensions of Fthaggua CIRCLE The Fire Vampires "). The Shoggoths, meanwhile, are a rather unique case, in that they were created by the Elder Things ( HPL At the Mountains of Madness ) but chose instead to venerate and serve Ubbo-Sathla EXP The Descent into the Abyss In " Dreams in the House of Weir ", by Lin Carter, the Dholes are explicitly called Shub-Niggurath 's "servitors", while the Nug-Soth are only identified as her "worshipers", possibly implying a distinction. Despite venerating the same deity, these two species are blood enemies.

--- Solar System.txt ---

[5] Cxaxukluth L495
This gas giant is inhabited by a strange species of fungoid creatures known as Yakshians, of which little is known. Several cosmic entities have made their home there over the aeons. A race called the Neptunians or Yakshians dwells on Neptune. ( HPL The Whisperer in Darkness CIRCLE The Family Tree of the Gods ") They might correspond to the spots of "hellish white fungi" that Randolph Carter observed on Neptune in his return to the solar system. ( HPL Through the Gates of the Silver Key For a brief period the Great Old One Hziulquoigmnzhah resided upon Yaksh after fleeing Yuggoth and the cannibalistic depredations of its parent Cxaxukluth . There it was worshiped by the Yakshians as a god, though it soon grew tired of these creatures and migrated to the planet Cykranosh. ( CIRCLE The Family Tree of the Gods Later, Hziulquoigmnzhah's nephew Tsathoggua (who was also escaping Cxaxukluth's attentions) and a female entity named Shathak met, mated and spawned the creature known as Zvilpogghua on Yaksh.

[6] Cxaxukluth L507
( HPL Through the Gates of the Silver Key For a brief period the Great Old One Hziulquoigmnzhah resided upon Yaksh after fleeing Yuggoth and the cannibalistic depredations of its parent Cxaxukluth . There it was worshiped by the Yakshians as a god, though it soon grew tired of these creatures and migrated to the planet Cykranosh. ( CIRCLE The Family Tree of the Gods Later, Hziulquoigmnzhah's nephew Tsathoggua (who was also escaping Cxaxukluth's attentions) and a female entity named Shathak met, mated and spawned the creature known as Zvilpogghua on Yaksh. However, Tsathoggua moved to Cykranosh on his journey to Earth, and Zvilpogghua left our solar system entirely, making its way to the planet Yrautrom in the Perseus constellation ( EXP The Feaster from the Stars Strange Manuscript Found in the Vermont Woods "). Whether Shathak remains in residence on Yaksh is unknown. Pluto (Yuggoth) Pluto is a dwarf planet. According to some accounts, Yuggoth , a colony of the Mi-Go in the solar system, is Pluto.


--- Solar_System_The_H.P._Lovecraft_Wiki_Fandom_ef7866cabd.txt ---

[5] Cxaxukluth L335
This gas giant is inhabited by a strange species of fungoid creatures known as Yakshians, of which little is known. Several cosmic entities have made their home there over the aeons. A race called the Neptunians or Yakshians dwells on Neptune. (HPL: The Whisperer in Darkness, CIRCLE: "The Family Tree of the Gods") They might correspond to the spots of "hellish white fungi" that Randolph Carter observed on Neptune in his return to the solar system. (HPL: "Through the Gates of the Silver Key") For a brief period the Great Old One Hziulquoigmnzhah resided upon Yaksh after fleeing Yuggoth and the cannibalistic depredations of its parent Cxaxukluth. There it was worshiped by the Yakshians as a god, though it soon grew tired of these creatures and migrated to the planet Cykranosh. (CIRCLE: "The Family Tree of the Gods") Later, Hziulquoigmnzhah's nephew Tsathoggua (who was also escaping Cxaxukluth's attentions) and a female entity named Shathak met, mated and spawned the creature known as Zvilpogghua on Yaksh.

[6] Cxaxukluth L337
(HPL: "Through the Gates of the Silver Key") For a brief period the Great Old One Hziulquoigmnzhah resided upon Yaksh after fleeing Yuggoth and the cannibalistic depredations of its parent Cxaxukluth. There it was worshiped by the Yakshians as a god, though it soon grew tired of these creatures and migrated to the planet Cykranosh. (CIRCLE: "The Family Tree of the Gods") Later, Hziulquoigmnzhah's nephew Tsathoggua (who was also escaping Cxaxukluth's attentions) and a female entity named Shathak met, mated and spawned the creature known as Zvilpogghua on Yaksh. However, Tsathoggua moved to Cykranosh on his journey to Earth, and Zvilpogghua left our solar system entirely, making its way to the planet Yrautrom in the Perseus constellation (EXP: "The Feaster from the Stars", "Strange Manuscript Found in the Vermont Woods"). Whether Shathak remains in residence on Yaksh is unknown. Pluto (Yuggoth)[] Pluto is a dwarf planet. According to some accounts, Yuggoth, a colony of the Mi-Go in the solar system, is Pluto.


--- Star-Stones (Cthulhu Universalis) [Star-Stones_(Cthulhu_Universalis)_a61f875c7c.txt].txt ---

[1] Lloigor L1145
Or perhaps the "astral self" of the star-stone travels with them to Celaeno, so as to be able to protect them "astrally"? Meaning of the Shape Clithanus wrote that "the five points [mark] the directions of the earth and the secret place beyond the earth from which the things of Evil had first come" [ Depths online text )] . The "secret place" might be in the constellation Orion , where Rigel and Betelgeuse were the source of the Elder Gods (1) and their slaves such as Lloigor and Zhar , according to Dr. Fo-Lan Lair ]. Or it could be a reference to the constellation Taurus , home of the Hyades and source of Hastur Island Keeper ]. The star-stones' shape resembles the traditional pentagram that is much used in magic spells. Variants There are some instances of stones that resemble star-stones but have an overall different shape. Seneca Lapham had two ovoid-shaped grey stones, each small enough to affix to a bracelet or armband.

--- Stories_d24568c9f1 [Frank Belknap Long, The Horror from the Hills.txt].txt ---

[10] Nut L2937
Competent scholars have traced, in a rough way, the advance or retrogression of racial groups in ethical and esthetic directions merely by studying and comparing their objects of worship and there does not exist a more fruitful science than idolography. "But occasionally our ethnologist encounters a nut that he cannot crack, a god or goddess so diabolical or grotesque or loathsome in conformation that it is impossible to link it associatively with even the most revolting of tribal retrogressions. It is a notorious fact that human races are less apt to advance than circle back on the course of evolution, and that idols and fetishes that were originally conceived in a comparatively noble spirit very often become, in the course of time, embodiments of the bestial and the obscene.


--- Stories_ee1c9b4b09 [August Derleth, The Seal of R'lyeh.txt].txt ---

[8] Lloigor L1755
Within two weeks we were off to Ponape in a chartered craft, off on that mission of which we dared breathe no word to the ship’s crew, for fear they would think us mad and desert. We were confident that our quest would be successful, that somewhere in the uncharted islands of the Polynesias we would find that which we sought, and, finding it, go to join forever our brethren of the seas who serve and wait upon the day of the resurrection, when Cthulhu and Hastur and Lloigor and Yog-Sothoth shall rise again and vanquish the Elder Gods in that titanic struggle which must come. We made Ponape our headquarters. Sometimes we set out from there; sometimes we used the craft we had chartered, oblivious of the curiosity of the crew. We searched the waters; sometimes we were gone for days. And soon my metamorphosis was complete.

--- THE_DISCIPLES_OF_CTHULHU,_edited_47967ee27e.txt ---

[2] Lloigor L43
A. Attanasio's "Glimpses" is an amazing work, a successful blending of Mythos horror and the science fictional themes he loves so well! In fact, not being a particular fan of science fiction myself, I'd be hard-pressed to try to describe just what happens in the story. But I think a hard science fiction fan would enjoy it immensely! Price's "Dope War of the Back Tong" I mentioned before, but let me just say that though the tale deals with the Tcho-Tcho cult that worships Lloigor and Zhar from Derleth and Schorer's "Lair of the Star-Spawn," the tale is not without its element of humor. Not that it's a parody or anything, but it does contain some very BAD puns! Eddy C. Bertin's "Darkness, My Name Is" is a piece no Mythos fan should miss! This atmospheric tale attempts to turn the countryside of Germany into something akin to Lovecraft's Arkham, or especially Dunwich, and succeeds admirably!
